QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #23 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hey Guys, I have the urge to let some steam out and come clean... I just feel like this is the right place to share since I know I am going to be sticking around until I either make it or https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal 40/45 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - RTM Community make it :) EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME A little history about me, I have been at this business for more years than I care to admit openly (cause you'll guess my age ), let's just say it's been over a decade I am working on becoming consistently successful in this path I have chosen for myself. I had a career in a very demanding industry and through hard work I even managed to carve my own partnership until many years later I felt I wasn't giving trading the attention it required so I closed shop and took it onto myself to make a living out of trading. I thought I would force myself to learn swimming or drown. That was 4 and half years ago... I had ups and downs as one would expect. Like so many, I too have gone down the indicator avenue and back up again until coming back full circle. I have had a lot of success at times and when my confidence grew I lost almost all in just a few trades. Suddenly the system I had built for myself that had generated 330% ROE in 6 months (with almost no drawdown) cost me 275% in 7 trades. I'll let you imagine what that did to my psyche... I then couldn't look at a chart for months on end without getting panic attacks; I had anxiety, depression and sleepless nights coupled with nightmares of market crashes (or rallies for that matter) that sucked the life out of me while of course being against me. The next phase was filled with research, learning Supply and Demand and of course after demoing for a few weeks, I jumped right back into live, losing the rest of my initial equity and much much more. It seemed every level I traded failed! It was as if there was this dark cloud of bad luck hanging over my head. Needless to say, I was devouring all Red's material, followed PIE, then registered with AG until I came here because IF was anyway my favorite all along (this is no flattery, it's true). I lurked for over a year in here absorbing the material and getting lost often along the way too; traded my new discovered knowledge only to lose more. I think I am under pressure because I need to make a living out of this. I am not giving up, even though lately I had to move back in with my family because I could not sustain the expenses of my own place. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal 41/45 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - RTM Community The reason for all the above is really because I caught myself lately looking into snake HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT oil again. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #34 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Area 3 The relevance of the broken lows at points 1, 2, & 3 are respectively associated with the breaks at A, B & C. Therefor on this TF they hold lots of weight compared to other ones that can be mostly considered local breaks. Point 4 is as was discussed earlier the last FL before the big drop, thus the most important zone in this rally into Area 2. The SR shows great history in several areas in the past, the most prominent one being 20/69 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community the last time it acted as resistance before the big bullish candle that broke A (at X). The HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT SR also shows lots of importance if we were to see it on a line chart. When price broke down through all of 4, 3, 2, & 1, big NEW decisions were made along the way. Now, I think there are two types of decisions in the market, prime & secondary. The former would be the break of something important like FL, FTR, Swing, SR, SD etc... & the latter the decision to either respect that initial decision (with a FL/FTR) or to reject it (with an Engulf of the primary in the opposite direction). Our job here at RTM is to follow in the footsteps of the last primary decision made until proven wrong (I think!!!), of course while bearing in mind all of what we have so generously been taught i.e. knowing where we are in terms of HTFs S/D and acting in our best interest. As human beings first and traders second I believe our attention is drawn to chaos over harmony & clutter over order. The reason I say this is because I always seem to pick the wrong areas to trade from and suffer the consequences of this visual incompetence. In the following chart is an example; https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 21/69 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In this chart we have the base of our previously discussed Area 2. In it I have highlighted the two most important FLs (IMHO) that were responsible for holding price up throughout this zone of balance. The upper green one bounced price back up 8 times as is seen on this TF. Now we know that the more a zone is visited the more orders get depleted from it especially if no newer orders are placed there. What I mean by that last part is, if you look at point A, this candle went really deep into the zone and must have picked up a lot of the remaining orders in the zone if not all of them. I for one would have definitely thought it would break down on the next visit after A... but it does not... why??? Because newer orders were placed there again. Be they limit or market orders... doesn't matter... ITs bought there on two other occasions before finally breaking the green zone. So in other words, a zone is not broke b4 it is broke. After the green price cuts through the yellow. Look at that beauty of a WRB! Nice! Had this been real time I am not even sure I would have given the yellow importance over the green... I know I wouldn't have! Why look at all the action that took place in the green, isn't this the area to look for a juicy short for when & if price comes back... surely! .... WRONG!!! First price doesn't make it there, second, hell if I was an IT why would I want to sell in an obvious place such as the green? So I'm slowly coming to the conclusion that the most important areas could be those we pay very little attention to like the yellow one. I couldn't know that for sure until it did react (that is something I am drilling into my head). Just look at the history of this SR/QM level! Notice the orange arrows in the past there. There was some serious buying. Point A is the low of 2008 i.e. the lowest point price got to during the crash of that year. Point B is the low of 2009, and as is clear during the beginning of that year when price came into the zone of the low of A, there were buyers. Why? Because it was last year's low zone from which point price rallied, so people bought again. Isn't' that text book herd behavior? When price visits those levels again what is the expectation of the ITs? They'll know that there surely will be buyers around those levels following the "buy low, sell high" advice no? Also wouldn't there be break out traders salivating over price going down to hell? Surely... What happens next is buyers & sellers get tripped for their stops, the ITs fill their huge orders and price rallies. This can be clearly seen through the candles 1,2 & 3. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 57/69 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT On this TF we also see that the drop in A cleared all the demand down to A, engulfing the previous FTR at X which implies that SHOULD (not a necessity) price ever cross below A it will go down to the next FTR in line. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #88 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Morning Robbie, Well I started out trading a very long time ago and had mixed results. Of course over the years I made and busted accounts. Throughout all of that there were days where I just thought I should just give up; but I did not. Then a few years back I read one of Cloggie's posts on FF where he recomended Red's PIE thread. That's where I came accross IF and the older gang and followed him to AG and then here. What RTM teaches are the nuts and bolts of trading but it is up to the individual to make it their own. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 24/42 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT A few months ago I read two books that have totally changed my perspective. One is "Trading in the Zone" and the other is "Flash boys". IMHO, having expectations while trading is the most dangerous minset of all. Micheal Douglas explains it all in his book. If you haven't read it, I reccomend you do. Even if all you gather from it is miniscule, it will reinforce proper trading behavior. In the previous post I mentioned my average was 18%, I've had days where I was making almost 100% while never risking more than 1% per trade. Many trades were also taken with a 0.25% risk. I had many off days as well, so yeah I'm content with the results. I have come to realize that pending orders are not for me. I want to be there while everyone else is working, and finish my day without any running trades. I just sleep better that way. My typical trading day is 13hrs, except for the days when I am satisfied with a certain return and there's lots of news coming up, I will shut down early. Yes I don't trade the actual event, I might get into something a few minutes after the dust settles but that's it. Don't stress yourself with expectation percentages, dollar ammounts or otherwise. They will only block your potential. Find what works for you and what doesn't. Trust yourself fully and treat each trade individually. If your stop hits, don't upset yourself and revenge trade. When you enter a trade you know that there is a probability that the SL can hit, that's why you placed it there in the first place. In the market, anything can happen at anytime. Hope this helps, Cheers The following user(s) said Thank You: Yofi, lv5758, Rdk123, Whenever, staurum 25/42 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community Lessons never stop coming... QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Pucci's Journal #96 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hi Robbie, Yes those were big decision areas. There is no "Price should or shouldn't" (IMHO) in trading. The only thing there is, is that price will do as it pleases. Our job is to try and tag along for the ride. Our signals come from all that is here on RTM. Each one of us trades these brilliant set of works differently. I for example am a true believer in "The Now"; i.e. I adapt to the current flow while paying attention to where we are. We could be in an amazing looking supply zone, I won't sell unless I see bigger fish biting first. Then it is all a matter of probability and MM. No two signals/opportunities are ever the same, they might be similar but never identical. Therefore; we are to keep an open/flexible mind and always be ready to change our orders with the current changes that are happening. For instance, I was scalping heavy buys in the first half of yesterday until the tide changed and I started selling. Granted, I am trading a lot of contracts during the day and closing at first or second TP on STFs. It's working for me for now and if a day comes where I'll need to tweak that, I will. Hope that helps, Cheers The following user(s) said Thank You: Ywicked, Rdk123, MJfx 36/42 Lessons never stop coming... https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #97 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 This is becoming a typical morning for me. LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #102 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hello Robbie, All of the trades I take are either FTR setups or reverse FTRs. The latter is nothing but an ignored higher low or lower high. The trick here is to give the trades enough room to breath while it may go against you a bit, and more importantly to close out early. A lot of times my RR is not really a good one per RR rules. That's because I will mostly close at the first area of opposition. I have deleted from my system the notion of "Price will/should break" because we are coming from HTF SD or what have you. In the event it does break I will re-enter. It is indeed tiresome to be watching and micromanaging entries and exist. But the advantage is really no more than a couple of hours work per day. I often trade until London closes but that is not really necessary as most days I make my quota even before London opens. For example, today I made 12% in two hours. I could call it a day or maybe not, we'll see. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #128 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=125 4/34 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hi Robbie, For simplicity's sake let's suppose we have only one TF, this is how I define the following: FL = RBR or DBD FTR= RBD or DBR Do you understand Harry's statement "The Flag Limit is an FTR. But not all FTRs are flag limits"??? Do you understand IF's statement "Sometimes we have to consider the whole candle as a FL/decison"??? If you haven't come across these yet, or maybe you may have accidentally dismissed their importance, go back to them and study them. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #131 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hi Robbie, You're not irritating me at all, on the contrary https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=125 9/34 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT As it was once explained to me, as price is going up for instance it will keep on making FL after FL. Now those maybe local FLs. The importance of an engulfed FL is the continuation of price in the direction of that engulf thus creating a new IMPORTANT FL. As you may know we have important and non-important FL. A FL may get engulfed several times, but if I don't see a continuation in the direction of any of the engulfs I will not touch that. The question I posted in Art's thread is not related because we had CP on approach after the ENG, thus a trade could be considered before a LL was printed. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #34 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Area 3 The relevance of the broken lows at points 1, 2, & 3 are respectively associated with the breaks at A, B & C. Therefor on this TF they hold lots of weight compared to other ones that can be mostly considered local breaks. Point 4 is as was discussed earlier the last FL before the big drop, thus the most important zone in this rally into Area 2. The SR shows great history in several areas in the past, the most prominent one being 20/69 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community the last time it acted as resistance before the big bullish candle that broke A (at X). The HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT SR also shows lots of importance if we were to see it on a line chart. When price broke down through all of 4, 3, 2, & 1, big NEW decisions were made along the way. Now, I think there are two types of decisions in the market, prime & secondary. The former would be the break of something important like FL, FTR, Swing, SR, SD etc... & the latter the decision to either respect that initial decision (with a FL/FTR) or to reject it (with an Engulf of the primary in the opposite direction). Our job here at RTM is to follow in the footsteps of the last primary decision made until proven wrong (I think!!!), of course while bearing in mind all of what we have so generously been taught i.e. knowing where we are in terms of HTFs S/D and acting in our best interest. As human beings first and traders second I believe our attention is drawn to chaos over harmony & clutter over order. The reason I say this is because I always seem to pick the wrong areas to trade from and suffer the consequences of this visual incompetence. In the following chart is an example; https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 21/69 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In this chart we have the base of our previously discussed Area 2. In it I have highlighted the two most important FLs (IMHO) that were responsible for holding price up throughout this zone of balance. The upper green one bounced price back up 8 times as is seen on this TF. Now we know that the more a zone is visited the more orders get depleted from it especially if no newer orders are placed there. What I mean by that last part is, if you look at point A, this candle went really deep into the zone and must have picked up a lot of the remaining orders in the zone if not all of them. I for one would have definitely thought it would break down on the next visit after A... but it does not... why??? Because newer orders were placed there again. Be they limit or market orders... doesn't matter... ITs bought there on two other occasions before finally breaking the green zone. So in other words, a zone is not broke b4 it is broke. After the green price cuts through the yellow. Look at that beauty of a WRB! Nice! Had this been real time I am not even sure I would have given the yellow importance over the green... I know I wouldn't have! Why look at all the action that took place in the green, isn't this the area to look for a juicy short for when & if price comes back... surely! .... WRONG!!! First price doesn't make it there, second, hell if I was an IT why would I want to sell in an obvious place such as the green? So I'm slowly coming to the conclusion that the most important areas could be those we pay very little attention to like the yellow one. I couldn't know that for sure until it did react (that is something I am drilling into my head). Just look at the history of this SR/QM level! Notice the orange arrows in the past there. There was some serious buying. Point A is the low of 2008 i.e. the lowest point price got to during the crash of that year. Point B is the low of 2009, and as is clear during the beginning of that year when price came into the zone of the low of A, there were buyers. Why? Because it was last year's low zone from which point price rallied, so people bought again. Isn't' that text book herd behavior? When price visits those levels again what is the expectation of the ITs? They'll know that there surely will be buyers around those levels following the "buy low, sell high" advice no? Also wouldn't there be break out traders salivating over price going down to hell? Surely... What happens next is buyers & sellers get tripped for their stops, the ITs fill their huge orders and price rallies. This can be clearly seen through the candles 1,2 & 3. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 57/69 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT On this TF we also see that the drop in A cleared all the demand down to A, engulfing the previous FTR at X which implies that SHOULD (not a necessity) price ever cross below A it will go down to the next FTR in line. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #88 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Morning Robbie, Well I started out trading a very long time ago and had mixed results. Of course over the years I made and busted accounts. Throughout all of that there were days where I just thought I should just give up; but I did not. Then a few years back I read one of Cloggie's posts on FF where he recomended Red's PIE thread. That's where I came accross IF and the older gang and followed him to AG and then here. What RTM teaches are the nuts and bolts of trading but it is up to the individual to make it their own. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 24/42 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT A few months ago I read two books that have totally changed my perspective. One is "Trading in the Zone" and the other is "Flash boys". IMHO, having expectations while trading is the most dangerous minset of all. Micheal Douglas explains it all in his book. If you haven't read it, I reccomend you do. Even if all you gather from it is miniscule, it will reinforce proper trading behavior. In the previous post I mentioned my average was 18%, I've had days where I was making almost 100% while never risking more than 1% per trade. Many trades were also taken with a 0.25% risk. I had many off days as well, so yeah I'm content with the results. I have come to realize that pending orders are not for me. I want to be there while everyone else is working, and finish my day without any running trades. I just sleep better that way. My typical trading day is 13hrs, except for the days when I am satisfied with a certain return and there's lots of news coming up, I will shut down early. Yes I don't trade the actual event, I might get into something a few minutes after the dust settles but that's it. Don't stress yourself with expectation percentages, dollar ammounts or otherwise. They will only block your potential. Find what works for you and what doesn't. Trust yourself fully and treat each trade individually. If your stop hits, don't upset yourself and revenge trade. When you enter a trade you know that there is a probability that the SL can hit, that's why you placed it there in the first place. In the market, anything can happen at anytime. Hope this helps, Cheers The following user(s) said Thank You: Yofi, lv5758, Rdk123, Whenever, staurum 25/42 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community Lessons never stop coming... QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Pucci's Journal #96 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hi Robbie, Yes those were big decision areas. There is no "Price should or shouldn't" (IMHO) in trading. The only thing there is, is that price will do as it pleases. Our job is to try and tag along for the ride. Our signals come from all that is here on RTM. Each one of us trades these brilliant set of works differently. I for example am a true believer in "The Now"; i.e. I adapt to the current flow while paying attention to where we are. We could be in an amazing looking supply zone, I won't sell unless I see bigger fish biting first. Then it is all a matter of probability and MM. No two signals/opportunities are ever the same, they might be similar but never identical. Therefore; we are to keep an open/flexible mind and always be ready to change our orders with the current changes that are happening. For instance, I was scalping heavy buys in the first half of yesterday until the tide changed and I started selling. Granted, I am trading a lot of contracts during the day and closing at first or second TP on STFs. It's working for me for now and if a day comes where I'll need to tweak that, I will. Hope that helps, Cheers The following user(s) said Thank You: Ywicked, Rdk123, MJfx 36/42 Lessons never stop coming... https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #97 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 This is becoming a typical morning for me. LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #102 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hello Robbie, All of the trades I take are either FTR setups or reverse FTRs. The latter is nothing but an ignored higher low or lower high. The trick here is to give the trades enough room to breath while it may go against you a bit, and more importantly to close out early. A lot of times my RR is not really a good one per RR rules. That's because I will mostly close at the first area of opposition. I have deleted from my system the notion of "Price will/should break" because we are coming from HTF SD or what have you. In the event it does break I will re-enter. It is indeed tiresome to be watching and micromanaging entries and exist. But the advantage is really no more than a couple of hours work per day. I often trade until London closes but that is not really necessary as most days I make my quota even before London opens. For example, today I made 12% in two hours. I could call it a day or maybe not, we'll see. Below is the P&L chart of this morning: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=100 3/33 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In the past year I must have sat in front of FT2 over 18hrs a day. Sometimes I'd get no sleep whatsoever. I must be thicker than all the rest on here cause there was always something I couldn't apply. Then it dawned on me to focus on one setup, and that's exactly what I did. Now I do recognize all the different ones as well, but I don't' necessarily trade them. I stick with my setup in thick and thin. Yes I do get stopped out like anyone else in this business but it usually is nothing more than a tiny scratch. As you mentioned, patience is the key. So is trading every one of your setups religiously, whether they get stopped out or not. Posts: 218 Thank you received: 253 pucci wrote: Hello Robbie, All of the trades I take are either FTR setups or reverse FTRs. The latter is nothing but an ignored higher low or lower high. The trick here is to give the trades enough room to breath while it may go against you a bit, and more importantly to close out early. A lot of times my RR is not really a good one per RR rules. That's because I will mostly close at the first area of opposition. I have deleted from my system the notion of "Price will/should break" because we are coming from HTF SD or what have you. In the event it does break I will re-enter. This has been my downfall up to this point as well! HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #131 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hi Robbie, You're not irritating me at all, on the contrary https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=125 9/34 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT As it was once explained to me, as price is going up for instance it will keep on making FL after FL. Now those maybe local FLs. The importance of an engulfed FL is the continuation of price in the direction of that engulf thus creating a new IMPORTANT FL. As you may know we have important and non-important FL. A FL may get engulfed several times, but if I don't see a continuation in the direction of any of the engulfs I will not touch that. The question I posted in Art's thread is not related because we had CP on approach after the ENG, thus a trade could be considered before a LL was printed. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #34 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Area 3 The relevance of the broken lows at points 1, 2, & 3 are respectively associated with the breaks at A, B & C. Therefor on this TF they hold lots of weight compared to other ones that can be mostly considered local breaks. Point 4 is as was discussed earlier the last FL before the big drop, thus the most important zone in this rally into Area 2. The SR shows great history in several areas in the past, the most prominent one being 20/69 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community the last time it acted as resistance before the big bullish candle that broke A (at X). The HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT SR also shows lots of importance if we were to see it on a line chart. When price broke down through all of 4, 3, 2, & 1, big NEW decisions were made along the way. Now, I think there are two types of decisions in the market, prime & secondary. The former would be the break of something important like FL, FTR, Swing, SR, SD etc... & the latter the decision to either respect that initial decision (with a FL/FTR) or to reject it (with an Engulf of the primary in the opposite direction). Our job here at RTM is to follow in the footsteps of the last primary decision made until proven wrong (I think!!!), of course while bearing in mind all of what we have so generously been taught i.e. knowing where we are in terms of HTFs S/D and acting in our best interest. As human beings first and traders second I believe our attention is drawn to chaos over harmony & clutter over order. The reason I say this is because I always seem to pick the wrong areas to trade from and suffer the consequences of this visual incompetence. In the following chart is an example; https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 21/69 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In this chart we have the base of our previously discussed Area 2. In it I have highlighted the two most important FLs (IMHO) that were responsible for holding price up throughout this zone of balance. The upper green one bounced price back up 8 times as is seen on this TF. Now we know that the more a zone is visited the more orders get depleted from it especially if no newer orders are placed there. What I mean by that last part is, if you look at point A, this candle went really deep into the zone and must have picked up a lot of the remaining orders in the zone if not all of them. I for one would have definitely thought it would break down on the next visit after A... but it does not... why??? Because newer orders were placed there again. Be they limit or market orders... doesn't matter... ITs bought there on two other occasions before finally breaking the green zone. So in other words, a zone is not broke b4 it is broke. After the green price cuts through the yellow. Look at that beauty of a WRB! Nice! Had this been real time I am not even sure I would have given the yellow importance over the green... I know I wouldn't have! Why look at all the action that took place in the green, isn't this the area to look for a juicy short for when & if price comes back... surely! .... WRONG!!! First price doesn't make it there, second, hell if I was an IT why would I want to sell in an obvious place such as the green? So I'm slowly coming to the conclusion that the most important areas could be those we pay very little attention to like the yellow one. Here's the return to it 22/69 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Excuse my delay on the initial analysis of Area 3... back into it now... I couldn't know that for sure until it did react (that is something I am drilling into my head). Just look at the history of this SR/QM level! Notice the orange arrows in the past there. There was some serious buying. Point A is the low of 2008 i.e. the lowest point price got to during the crash of that year. Point B is the low of 2009, and as is clear during the beginning of that year when price came into the zone of the low of A, there were buyers. Why? Because it was last year's low zone from which point price rallied, so people bought again. Isn't' that text book herd behavior? When price visits those levels again what is the expectation of the ITs? They'll know that there surely will be buyers around those levels following the "buy low, sell high" advice no? Also wouldn't there be break out traders salivating over price going down to hell? Surely... What happens next is buyers & sellers get tripped for their stops, the ITs fill their huge orders and price rallies. This can be clearly seen through the candles 1,2 & 3. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=25 57/69 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT On this TF we also see that the drop in A cleared all the demand down to A, engulfing the previous FTR at X which implies that SHOULD (not a necessity) price ever cross below A it will go down to the next FTR in line. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #88 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Morning Robbie, Well I started out trading a very long time ago and had mixed results. Of course over the years I made and busted accounts. Throughout all of that there were days where I just thought I should just give up; but I did not. Then a few years back I read one of Cloggie's posts on FF where he recomended Red's PIE thread. That's where I came accross IF and the older gang and followed him to AG and then here. What RTM teaches are the nuts and bolts of trading but it is up to the individual to make it their own. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 24/42 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT A few months ago I read two books that have totally changed my perspective. One is "Trading in the Zone" and the other is "Flash boys". IMHO, having expectations while trading is the most dangerous minset of all. Micheal Douglas explains it all in his book. If you haven't read it, I reccomend you do. Even if all you gather from it is miniscule, it will reinforce proper trading behavior. In the previous post I mentioned my average was 18%, I've had days where I was making almost 100% while never risking more than 1% per trade. Many trades were also taken with a 0.25% risk. I had many off days as well, so yeah I'm content with the results. I have come to realize that pending orders are not for me. I want to be there while everyone else is working, and finish my day without any running trades. I just sleep better that way. My typical trading day is 13hrs, except for the days when I am satisfied with a certain return and there's lots of news coming up, I will shut down early. Yes I don't trade the actual event, I might get into something a few minutes after the dust settles but that's it. Don't stress yourself with expectation percentages, dollar ammounts or otherwise. They will only block your potential. Find what works for you and what doesn't. Trust yourself fully and treat each trade individually. If your stop hits, don't upset yourself and revenge trade. When you enter a trade you know that there is a probability that the SL can hit, that's why you placed it there in the first place. In the market, anything can happen at anytime. Hope this helps, Cheers The following user(s) said Thank You: Yofi, lv5758, Rdk123, Whenever, staurum 25/42 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community Lessons never stop coming... QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Pucci's Journal #96 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hi Robbie, Yes those were big decision areas. There is no "Price should or shouldn't" (IMHO) in trading. The only thing there is, is that price will do as it pleases. Our job is to try and tag along for the ride. Our signals come from all that is here on RTM. Each one of us trades these brilliant set of works differently. I for example am a true believer in "The Now"; i.e. I adapt to the current flow while paying attention to where we are. We could be in an amazing looking supply zone, I won't sell unless I see bigger fish biting first. Then it is all a matter of probability and MM. No two signals/opportunities are ever the same, they might be similar but never identical. Therefore; we are to keep an open/flexible mind and always be ready to change our orders with the current changes that are happening. For instance, I was scalping heavy buys in the first half of yesterday until the tide changed and I started selling. Granted, I am trading a lot of contracts during the day and closing at first or second TP on STFs. It's working for me for now and if a day comes where I'll need to tweak that, I will. Hope that helps, Cheers The following user(s) said Thank You: Ywicked, Rdk123, MJfx 36/42 Lessons never stop coming... https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=75 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #97 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 This is becoming a typical morning for me. LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #102 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hello Robbie, All of the trades I take are either FTR setups or reverse FTRs. The latter is nothing but an ignored higher low or lower high. The trick here is to give the trades enough room to breath while it may go against you a bit, and more importantly to close out early. A lot of times my RR is not really a good one per RR rules. That's because I will mostly close at the first area of opposition. I have deleted from my system the notion of "Price will/should break" because we are coming from HTF SD or what have you. In the event it does break I will re-enter. It is indeed tiresome to be watching and micromanaging entries and exist. But the advantage is really no more than a couple of hours work per day. I often trade until London closes but that is not really necessary as most days I make my quota even before London opens. For example, today I made 12% in two hours. I could call it a day or maybe not, we'll see. Below is the P&L chart of this morning: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=100 3/33 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In the past year I must have sat in front of FT2 over 18hrs a day. Sometimes I'd get no sleep whatsoever. I must be thicker than all the rest on here cause there was always something I couldn't apply. Then it dawned on me to focus on one setup, and that's exactly what I did. Now I do recognize all the different ones as well, but I don't' necessarily trade them. I stick with my setup in thick and thin. Yes I do get stopped out like anyone else in this business but it usually is nothing more than a tiny scratch. As you mentioned, patience is the key. So is trading every one of your setups religiously, whether they get stopped out or not. I am in no way affiliated or trying to promote Michael Douglas' book Trading in the Zone, but it was a game changer for me. It might not have the same effect on everyone else. It benefited me greatly and this is why I am mentioning it again. I hope I answered everything you meant to ask. Posts: 218 Thank you received: 253 pucci wrote: Hello Robbie, All of the trades I take are either FTR setups or reverse FTRs. The latter is nothing but an ignored higher low or lower high. The trick here is to give the trades enough room to breath while it may go against you a bit, and more importantly to close out early. A lot of times my RR is not really a good one per RR rules. That's because I will mostly close at the first area of opposition. I have deleted from my system the notion of "Price will/should break" because we are coming from HTF SD or what have you. In the event it does break I will re-enter. This has been my downfall up to this point as well! HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Pucci's Journal #131 pucci Offline Trader Posts: 197 Thank you received: 333 Hi Robbie, You're not irritating me at all, on the contrary https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1927-pucci-s-journal?start=125 9/34 7/27/2018 Pucci's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT As it was once explained to me, as price is going up for instance it will keep on making FL after FL. Now those maybe local FLs. The importance of an engulfed FL is the continuation of price in the direction of that engulf thus creating a new IMPORTANT FL. As you may know we have important and non-important FL. A FL may get engulfed several times, but if I don't see a continuation in the direction of any of the engulfs I will not touch that. The question I posted in Art's thread is not related because we had CP on approach after the ENG, thus a trade could be considered before a LL was printed.
